# PASS 1 (DESIGN) — `crimewidthkill`: an unsupported access width to the SGI IP32 CRIME controller ends the process

VERDICT LINE FIRST (`<option-set member>` + one clause), then the liveness token `CRIME_P1_20260913`.

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this brief's author stated wrongly.  **Check the premises,
not just the derivations.**  Where I state something as fact, ask whether it is true.  A seat that re-derives a
premise from something this brief did NOT give it is worth more than a seat that agrees.

**THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`** (`C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\`).  Other copies of GXemul
under the project root are pristine 2021 upstream and are NOT this fork.  **Scope your reading to `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`
and `GXEMUL-SEC/regress/*.py`.**  If you cannot read files that is expected — every fact you need is inlined; name any
premise you cannot settle from this packet.  Where a claim is arithmetic (an offset, a byte lane, a count),
ENUMERATE it rather than reasoning about it.

## THE DEFECT, MEASURED (rung 3, before any edit)

`src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c`, `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)` — the IP32 memory and interrupt controller, registered
`DM_DEFAULT` at physical 0x14000000, length 0x280, backing store `uint64_t reg[0x280 / 8]` — sends every access whose
length is neither 8 nor 4 to `fatal("crime access len = %i!\n", len); exit(1);`.

**Measured** with a cold-debugger witness on the unmodified `-E sgi -e ip32` machine
(`regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/crime_width_witness_20260913.py`, output `witness_committed.out`): **3/15,
CONTROL=OK**.  The controls pass: a RAM round trip (`live`); a 4-byte read of CRIME_REV's low half at KSEG1 0xb4000004
returns the model's revision 0x11 (`devsig`); its high half at 0xb4000000 reads 0 (`devsig hi`).  Then **each of
`lb`, `lbu`, `lh`, `lhu` (reads) and `sb`, `sh` (writes) ends the process with exit status 1 and the text
`crime access len = N!`**, and the RAM round trip that follows never runs.  One guest byte access to the machine's
interrupt controller terminates the emulator.

## SETTLED PREMISES (each checkable; please check them)

1. **`fatal()` prints and RETURNS; `exit(1)` is the terminating call.**
2. **A device callback returning 0 becomes a GUEST EXCEPTION on MIPS.**  `src/cpus/memory_rw.c`, verbatim:
   ```c
   if (res == 0)
           res = -1;
   /*  If accessing the memory mapped device failed, then return with an exception.  */
   if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {
           debug("[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx failed ]\n", writeflag? "writing to" : "reading from", ...);
   #ifdef MEM_MIPS
           mips_cpu_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION? EXCEPTION_IBE : EXCEPTION_DBE, 0, vaddr, 0, 0, 0, 0);
   #endif
           return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
   }
   ```
   So option 5 (FAULT, `return 0`) is AVAILABLE on this CPU: the guest takes a data bus error, and the `debug()` line
   prints when single-stepping.  `machine_sgi.c` installs `dev_unreadable` (whose whole access function is
   `return 0;`) ten times, because PROMs and NetBSD probe absent regions and rely on the failure.
3. **The nearest in-tree precedent, SAME MACHINE, SAME KIND OF GUARD:** `src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c` (the CRIME
   rendering engine at 0x15000000) had six width guards of the same shape.  Rounds #497/#499/#513 made them
   **latched declines that reach the common tail**, e.g.:
   ```c
   if (len != 8) {
           if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN)) {
                   d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN;
                   fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for CRIME_RE_TLB_A -- declined.  (once) ]\n", (int) len);
           }
   } else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) { ...
   ```
   and that round's detector records `return 0` as a DIFFERENT disposition (it "turns into a guest EXCEPTION_DBE"),
   rejected there.  **Whether that choice transfers here is a question, not a premise** — RE is a graphics engine, CRIME
   is the memory/interrupt controller.
4. **A second precedent in THIS file:** `DEVICE_ACCESS(macepci)` opens with
   `if (len != 4) fatal("[ macepci: unimplemented len %i ]\n", len);` — an UNLATCHED announce, then it proceeds with
   the access.  No termination.
5. **`struct crime_data` has no latch field today** (`reg[]`, `last_microseconds`, `irq`, `prev_asserted`, `use_fb`).
   The house idiom for a guest-drivable diagnostic is a per-instance N = 1 latch with `(once)` in the text.
6. **No CRIME hardware documentation is present locally** (`_scratchpad/*.pdf` has none).  What real CRIME does on a
   byte or halfword access — serve a byte lane, bus-error, or something else — is NOT established here.
7. **Two OTHER terminating calls sit in the same function, above the width guard**, keyed on the MACHINE'S RAM SIZE
   (`physical_ram_in_mb > 1024`, or not a multiple of the bank size) — set by `-M` at launch, not by the guest.  They
   are NOT this round's subject; say so if you disagree.

## THE FULL ENCLOSING FUNCTION (verbatim from the tree; every exit path and the common tail)

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
{
	/*
	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
	 *  physical 0x14000000.
	 *
	 *  On my real O2:
	 *
	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *
	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
	 *  would work too.)
	 *
	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
	 */
	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
	size_t i;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  Set crime version/revision:
	 *
	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
	 *
	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
	 */
	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;

	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	/*
	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
	 *
	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
	 *  bank #0."
	 */
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int mb_per_bank;
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
		mb_per_bank = 32;
	else
		mb_per_bank = 128;

	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
	size_t total_mb = 0;
	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
		else
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;

		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
	}
	
	if (len == 8) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
		else
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
	} else if (len == 4) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			if (relative_addr & 4) {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
			} else {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
			}
		} else {
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
			if (relative_addr & 4)
				odata = (int32_t)odata;
			else
				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
		}
	} else {
		fatal("crime access len = %i!\n", len);
		exit(1);
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {

	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
		/*
		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
		 *
		 *  The address where this happens is:
		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
		 *
		 *  4.18 works too.
		 *
		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
		 */
		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
			if (cpu->is_32bit)
				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
			else
				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*
			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
			 */
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
			}
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
				int j;

				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
				    "reboot" command:  */
				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;

				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
			}

			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

			if (idata != 0)
				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
		break;

	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
		// No warning.
		break;

	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
		crime_update_crime_time(d);
		break;

	default:
		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
			    (int)relative_addr, len);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" ]\n");
		} else {
			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
		}
	}

	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```

## THE OPTION SET (seven members; is there an eighth?)

1 DELETE the two lines and fall through · 2 DECLINE silently · 3 DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE (the RE precedent) ·
4 ACCEPT (the model already computes the value) · 5 FAULT (`return 0` → guest data bus error; available on MIPS) ·
6 BACKED ACCEPT (invent storage) · 7 IMPLEMENT (serve narrow accesses by byte lane from the 64-bit register array).

Notes a seat should check, not trust:
* **Member 1 is not the same as member 2 here**, because the `switch (relative_addr)` BELOW the width guard has side
  effects.  If a narrow access merely skips the store/load and falls through, the switch still runs.  Enumerate what a
  1- or 2-byte access could reach in it: `CRIME_REV` (0x000) redirects the PC when read from the PROM's cache-probe
  loop; `CRIME_CONTROL` (0x008) on a WRITE tests `idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET` and stops every CPU — is that bit
  within a halfword's `idata`?; `CRIME_INTSTAT`/`CRIME_INTMASK` call `crime_interrupt_reassert()`; `CRIME_TIME` updates
  the time; the default arm prints `debug()`.  Which of these should a refused access trigger?
* **Member 7 needs a byte-lane rule.**  The 4-byte arm treats `reg[]` as big-endian halves (`relative_addr & 4` picks
  the LOW half).  A byte rule would extend that — but is a byte read of a 64-bit control register meaningful on real
  CRIME, and does any guest do it?
* **Member 5 vs 3** is the design question the filing raised: FAULT is "a positive statement that the access could
  not be serviced", delivered as the error real hardware would give — IF real hardware refuses narrow accesses.  With
  no documentation (premise 6), what warrant can each member claim?

## QUESTIONS

1. **Which member, and why?**  Check the warrant against the tree: grep the fork for the idiom, and for how the IP32
   PROM path and guest drivers in this tree reach CRIME.  If you find a guest or PROM that issues a narrow CRIME access,
   that settles a great deal — say where.
2. **Does a refused access run the `switch`?**  For your chosen member, say exactly which lines a 1- and a 2-byte read
   and write execute, including the common tail (`preserved_CRIME_HARDINT` restore, the `INTSTAT` recompute, and the
   `memory_writemax64` on a read).
3. **"What is the SMALLEST EDIT that breaks your fix and still passes"** a detector you would write?  Name the rows you
   would need: the witness's survival and text rows, the accept cells (4 and 8, read and write), the switch
   side-effect cells for a refused access, the value a refused read returns (FAULT leaves the destination untouched; a
   decline writes 0 through the tail — a sentinel-seeded destination tells them apart), and a latch count if member 3.
4. **The premise audit.**  Which settled premise above is wrong or incomplete?

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE — say so.

else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

far more to this panel than a timeout.

codex
Two premises need qualification: the bundled NetBSD header says “all registers 64-bit access,” so there is some local evidence about intended widths. Also, the ten `unreadable` registrations are split across machine branches; I’m checking which branches before treating them as an IP32 precedent. The width guard’s placement also matters because CRIME state is refreshed before it runs.

49:  *
50:  *  Read at most 64 bits of data from a buffer.  Length is given by
51:  *  len, and the byte order by cpu->byte_order.
52:  *
53:  *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
54:  */
55: uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
56: {
57: 	int i, byte_order = cpu->byte_order;
58: 	uint64_t x = 0;
59: 
60: 	if (len & MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {
61: 		len &= ~MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
62: 		byte_order = EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	/*  Switch byte order for incoming data, if necessary:  */
66: 	if (byte_order == EMUL_BIG_ENDIAN)
67: 		for (i=0; i<len; i++) {
68: 			x <<= 8;
69: 			x |= buf[i];
70: 		}
71: 	else
72: 		for (i=len-1; i>=0; i--) {
73: 			x <<= 8;
74: 			x |= buf[i];
75: 		}
76: 
77: 	return x;
78: }

#!/usr/bin/env python3
#
#  #497 widthkillclass -- the detector.
#
#  CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- which edits this detector is designed
#  to catch, and which it is NOT:
#
#    CATCHES
#      * removal or reinstatement of ANY of the six width kills, at any one of
#        the six sites individually (the `sw surv' rows are per site);
#      * narrowing the decline so it applies to writes only, i.e.
#        `len != 8' -> `len != 8 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE'  (the `lw surv' rows);
#      * ANY decline that does not reach the function's common tail -- an early
#        `return', or `return 0' (which memory_rw coerces to -1 and turns into a
#        guest EXCEPTION_DBE).  The `lw surv' rows check the VALUE the declined
#        read returns, not merely that the host survived: the destination
#        register is preset to a sentinel, so "tail ran, wrote 0" and "load
#        never completed" are distinguishable.  A survival-only oracle at a
#        refusal-effect site is vacuous by construction, and this row was one
#        until the d_returnzero mutant was built;
#      * deleting the latch, or dropping the `d->warned |= BIT' set, so the
#        message floods  (the `sw lat1' rows COUNT; they do not test presence);
#      * making two or more sites share a latch bit  (`xsite 6msg');
#      * mis-attributing a window's message to another window (`xsite order');
#      * growth of the width predicate that swallows LEGAL 8-byte accesses
#        (the `accept' rows, probed at an offset with bit 3 SET so that a
#        `|| (relative_addr & 8)' tightening reddens them);
#      * silent removal of the six READ kills or of the final catch-all, which
#        this round deliberately does NOT touch  (`<s> ld kill' x6, `other kill').
#
#    *** THE TWO PARAGRAPHS ABOVE ARE #497's AND #499's AND ARE NO LONGER TRUE OF THIS
#        FILE.  #513 REMOVED ALL SIX READ KILLS -- five arms became ACCEPTs and
#        CRIME_RE_TEX became a latched decline -- so `<s> ld kill' x6 NO LONGER EXIST
#        as row names, and an 8-byte `ld' to those windows no longer ends the host.
#        The contract below is the LIVE one; the text above is kept, with this
#        correction beside it, because a reader arriving from #497's or #499's records
#        needs to see which half moved and when.  Found by the pass-2 panel, which read
#        the contract against the diff and reported it stale. ***
#
#    *** AND IT PINS THE ROUND'S CENTRAL DISCLOSURE, RATHER THAN ASSERTING IT.
#        The width guard is evaluated FIRST, so after #497 a 4-byte read
#        DECLINES -- but an ordinary 8-byte `ld' to each of the six windows
#        STILL ENDS THE HOST, because len == 8 passes the width guard and falls
#        into the read arm's exit(1) (dev_sgi_re.c lines 245 262 279 296 346
#        362).  Fixing six width kills leaves EXACTLY six read kills at the same
#        six offsets.  The `<s> ld kill' rows measure that at every window, so a
#        future reader cannot conclude from `lw surv' that reads were made safe.
#        Filed as sgirekills.  *** ITS WITNESS IS THIS FILE: the six `<site> ld kill`
#        rows below ARE rung-3 symptom demonstrations of that defect, and the ledger's
#        witness field names them.  This comment used to say the row `has no witness
#        yet' -- written before those rows existed and left standing after they shipped,
#        so the file denied being the witness it had become.  Found by the codex seat. ***
#
#    DOES NOT CATCH
#      * anything in DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de) or (sgi_mte), including sgi_de's own
#        width kill (accept set {4,8}, filed sgidewidth) and sgi_mte's
#        unreachable one (filed sgimtewidth);
#      * the LINEAR out-of-bounds index itself.  Observing re_linear_b[0] being
#        clobbered needs a READ path, and every read arm here still exit(1)s.
#        *** THAT CLAUSE IS #497's AND IS FALSE SINCE #513: five read arms ACCEPT and
#        CRIME_RE_TEX declines, so a READ PATH NOW EXISTS.  Whether that makes the
#        aliasing rowable is a QUESTION FOR sgilinearoob, not a claim made here.
#        The aliasing is pinned by offsetof in PRETABLE.out section 1.2, not by
#        a row.  Owed to sgirekills.
#
#    ONE THING THE CONTRACT UNDERSTATED, corrected before shipping rather than
#    left as a modest-sounding false claim: an earlier draft said there is NO
#    anchored cell-position row.  There is.  Each `accept' row matches the arm's
#    own debug() text INCLUDING THE INDEX AND THE VALUE -- e.g.
#    "d->re_tlb_a[4] = 0x1122" -- so it is anchored to a SPECIFIC cell by a
#    channel outside the decode under test, which is clause (f)'s requirement.
#    A re-map of the index arithmetic (>> 1 -> >> 2) or of the byte packing
#    reddens it.  Window-level translations are additionally caught by the
#    per-site attribution rows, since each checks the message naming ITS OWN
#    symbol.  What remains genuinely uncovered is a re-map that preserves both
#    index and symbol.
#
import os, pty, re, select, sys, time

BIN   = sys.argv[1]

304: 		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
305: 		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
306: 		 *
307: 		 *  The address where this happens is:
308: 		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
309: 		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
310: 		 *
311: 		 *  4.18 works too.
312: 		 *
313: 		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
314: 		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
315: 		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
316: 		 */
317: 		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
318: 			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
319: 			if (cpu->is_32bit)
320: 				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
321: 			else
322: 				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
323: 		}
324: 		break;
325: 
326: 	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
327: 		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
328: 		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
329: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
330: 			/*
331: 			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
332: 			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
333: 			 */
334: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
335: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
336: 			}
337: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
338: 				int j;
339: 
340: 				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
341: 				    "reboot" command:  */
342: 				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
343: 					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
344: 
345: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
346: 			}
347: 
348: 			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
349: 
350: 			if (idata != 0)
351: 				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
352: 				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
353: 		}
354: 		break;
355: 
356: 	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
357: 	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
358: 		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
359: 		break;
360: 
361: 	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
362: 		// No warning.
363: 		break;
364: 
365: 	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */

367: 		break;

369: 	default:
370: 		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {

372: 			    (int)relative_addr, len);
373: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)

376: 		} else {
377: 			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
378: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
379: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
380: 			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);

384: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;

387: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

389: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
390: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

392: 	return 1;
459: 	case 32:
460: 		strlcat(machineName, " (O2)", MACHINE_NAME_MAXBUF);
461: 
462: 		/*
463: 		 *  The IP32 prom probes "bank 0" with the following
464: 		 *  TLB entries:
465: 		 *
466: 		 *  00: vaddr=0000000000000000 (global):  p0=0x040000000 D p1=0x041000000 D (16MB)
467: 		 *  01: vaddr=0000000002000000 (global):  p0=0x042000000 D p1=0x043000000 D (16MB)
468: 		 *  02: vaddr=0000000004000000 (global):  p0=0x044000000 D p1=0x045000000 D (16MB)
469: 		 *  03: vaddr=0000000006000000 (global):  p0=0x046000000 D p1=0x047000000 D (16MB)
470: 		 *
471: 		 *  In other words, it uses real memory at 0x40000000 and up.
472: 		 *  However, it is _also_ accessible at physical address 0x0.
473: 		 *
474: 		 *  Max amount of RAM in an O2 is 1 GB. However, devices start at
475: 		 *  0x14000000 (or below that), so 256 MB is the most that the legacy GXemul
476: 		 *  framework can handle. Memory above that must be accessed from 0x40000000 and up.
477:  		 */
478: 
479: 		if (machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
480: 			fatal("SGI O2 cannot have more than 1 GB of RAM.\n");
481: 			exit(1);
482: 		}
483: 
484: 		/*  In the new framework, this would be the other way around :-), i.e.
485: 		    actual memory at 0x40000000 and the first 256 MB would be mirrored
486: 		    to address 0.  */
487: 		{
488: 			int mirrorInMB = machine->physical_ram_in_mb < 256 ? machine->physical_ram_in_mb : 256;
489: 			dev_ram_init(machine, 0x40000000ULL, mirrorInMB * 1048576, DEV_RAM_MIRROR, 0, NULL);
490: 			if (machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 256)
491: 				dev_ram_init(machine, 0x50000000ULL, (machine->physical_ram_in_mb - 256) * 1048576, DEV_RAM_RAM, 0, NULL);
492: 		}

codex
The premise audit is complete: the ten `unreadable` call sites are unreachable behind existing aborts in IP20/IP22/IP24 setup, and none belongs to IP32. I still favor FAULT, with an entry guard before payload decoding and register refresh. The proposed checks need to verify the guest exception and CRIME state; process survival and diagnostic text alone would miss several incorrect fixes.

codex
5 FAULT — reject unsupported widths through the existing MIPS device-failure path.  
CRIME_P1_20260913

This is a model policy choice; the exact hardware response remains unestablished. FAULT reports that the model cannot service the access. Member 3 has a compatibility precedent, but successfully returning zero from a memory/interrupt controller also asserts behavior we have not established.

There is **local evidence omitted from premise 6**: [crimereg.h:39](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/crimereg.h:39) says **“all registers 64-bit access.”** That supports caution about implementing narrower widths, but does not establish the hardware’s rejection behavior. The existing 4-byte compatibility path should remain.

I found no guest driver or PROM instruction sequence in the inspected source establishing byte or halfword CRIME traffic. The relevant evidence was the emulator’s PROM workaround and its comment about NetBSD writing the control register at offset `0x00c`. Absence from this source search does not establish absence from real guests.

There is also a closer structural precedent than the six RE guards: [DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de)](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1182) accepts `{4,8}` and uses a latched decline that explicitly jumps over its switch and drawing operation. That supports skipping operational effects, although it does not settle FAULT versus successful decline.

An eighth combination is **FAULT + LATCHED ANNOUNCE**. Logging and bus disposition are separate choices. I would use member 5’s existing dispatcher diagnostic without introducing another device warning.

For the proposed design, place:

```c
if (len != 4 && len != 8)
	return 0;
```

after the local declarations at line 208, before payload decoding at line 210, and remove the obsolete terminating width arm. Preserve the accepted paths and their common tail.

This placement matters: replacing only the existing `exit(1)` would still execute the preceding REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, and bank-register refreshes.

| Refused operation | Executes inside `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)` |
|---|---|
| 1-byte read | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |
| 2-byte read | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |
| 1-byte write | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |
| 2-byte write | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |

For all four, payload decoding, register refreshes, array access, and the switch are skipped. **None of the common tail executes:** no HARDINT restore, no INTSTAT recomputation, and no `memory_writemax64`. The callback has made no state changes needing cleanup.

The RAM-check statements remain, although unsupported widths now return before reaching them. Those checks remain a separate configuration concern.

For ordinary MIPS data accesses, the dispatcher raises DBE and the load implementation returns before destination-register writeback. However, **“destination untouched” must mean the guest GPR**: this fork [zeroes the transfer buffer before invoking the callback](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485). A sentinel in that buffer cannot distinguish dispositions when testing through `memory_rw`.

The switch effects that member 1 would retain are:

| Exact relative offset | Effect if an unsupported width falls through |
|---|---|
| `0x000`, REV | Redirects the PC in the selected PROM PC window on **reads and writes**. There is no direction check or “second read” counter. |
| `0x008`, CONTROL | Reads do nothing in this arm. Writes inspect raw `idata`, potentially stop CPUs, and may announce unimplemented control bits. |
| `0x010`, INTSTAT | Reads and writes call `crime_interrupt_reassert`. |
| `0x018`, INTMASK | Reads and writes call `crime_interrupt_reassert`. |
| `0x030`, DOG | Breaks without an additional action. |
| `0x038`, TIME | Reads and writes call `crime_update_crime_time`. |
| Other offsets | Execute the default debug output. |

The control-bit arithmetic is explicit: a byte supplies bits `7..0`, maximum `0x00ff`; a halfword supplies bits `15..0`, maximum `0xffff`; HARD_RESET is `0x0800`, bit 11. **A halfword can satisfy that test; a byte cannot.** The switch consumes raw decoded `idata`, without first positioning it into a register byte lane.

These are exact-address cases: `0x004` does not enter REV, and `0x00c` does not enter CONTROL. None of these switch effects belongs on the proposed refused path.

For a detector, I would require the following rows:

- **Controls and survival:** retain `live`, `devsig`, and `devsig hi`; enumerate `lb`, `lbu`, `lh`, `lhu`, `sb`, and `sh` separately. Each needs process survival, absence of the old terminating diagnostic, and a subsequent RAM round trip **after controlled exception recovery**. FAULT intentionally prevents ordinary sequential continuation until the exception is handled.
- **Fault disposition:** check DBE, the faulting EPC under controlled initial exception state, and an unchanged nonzero destination GPR for each of the four loads. Under stepping, check the dispatcher’s failure diagnostic. A surviving process or unchanged GPR alone is insufficient.
- **Accepted accesses:** enumerate 4-byte high-half read, low-half read, high-half write, low-half write, 8-byte read, and 8-byte write. Offset `+0` selects bits `63..32`; `+4` selects bits `31..0`. Use distinct halves, verify preservation of the other half, and check accepted operations both before and after refusals. Include positive checks for HARDINT restoration and INTSTAT recomputation.
- **Refused switch effects:** exercise `{1-read, 2-read, 1-write, 2-write}` at every switch case and a default offset. Observe REV redirection, CPU running flags, interrupt assertion/deassertion, time state, and diagnostics. Prime the interrupt fixture so an erroneous reassert would be observable; control ticks and time so background updates cannot hide or imitate the result.
- **Guard placement:** snapshot CRIME fields directly before and after refusal. Subsequent ordinary CRIME reads themselves refresh several fields and can conceal an incorrectly late guard.
- **Diagnostic policy:** member 5 has no new latch count. If member 3 or the eighth combination is selected, require exactly one announcement per instance across repeated widths and directions, plus an independent announcement from a second instance.

The smallest obvious edit escaping **survival-only** checks is `return 0` → `return 1`. The GPR and DBE rows catch it.

A **one-operator edit that survives all tests restricted to widths `{1,2,4,8}`** is changing the new guard’s first `len != 4` to `len < 4`. Widths 5, 6, and 7 then escape refusal. Thus the callback-level detector also needs unsupported-length cases outside the instruction witness: enumerate 3, 5, 6, 7, and a representative length above 8.

There is another detector limitation worth making explicit: because the dispatcher pre-zeroes read buffers, a successful early `return 1` can look like “the common tail returned zero.” **A guest zero-value row proves successful zero delivery, not execution of the common tail.** Direct callback tests with a seeded buffer distinguish those implementations when tail execution is part of the contract.

The settled-premise audit is:

| Premise | Finding |
|---|---|
| **1** | Confirmed. [fatal](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c:384) prints through `va_debug` and returns. |
| **2** | The normal MIPS exception path is confirmed, with `!no_exceptions` qualification. The “installs ten times” claim is wrong as a runtime statement; see below. |
| **3** | The six RE width declines are present: TLB_A, TLB_B, TLB_C, TEX, LINEAR_A, LINEAR_B. The DE guard is an additional, closer control-flow precedent. |
| **4** | Confirmed: MACEPCI announces and proceeds. This establishes existing behavior, not suitable CRIME semantics. |
| **5** | Confirmed: CRIME has no latch field; the per-instance warning idiom exists. That convention does not itself select a bus disposition. |
| **6** | Incomplete: the bundled header supplies width-related evidence. Exact hardware behavior remains unsettled; PDF absence outside the permitted reading scope was not verified. |
| **7** | Agreed: the RAM failures are outside this width round. RAM size also comes from defaults/configuration, not exclusively `-M`. Setup already rejects values above 1024 MB before registering CRIME. |

Specifically, the ten `unreadable` sites enumerate as:

- IP20: lines **241, 242, 245, 246, 247**, following an unconditional `abort()` at line 220.
- Shared IP22/IP24 branch: lines **345, 346, 349, 350, 351**, following subtype-specific unconditional aborts at lines 268 and 276.
- IP32: **no such registration**.

These are ten textual call sites, not ten live installations in this fork. [machine_sgi.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_sgi.c:219)

Two additional findings affect follow-up work. The MIPS exception implementation does not update BadVAddr for DBE, so that should not be a required fault-address oracle here. Also, the bank-register synthesis disagrees with the bundled header:

| Bank | Loop offset | Header offset |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `0x208` | `0x208` |
| 1 | `0x210` | `0x218` |
| 2 | `0x218` | `0x210` |
| 3 | `0x220` | `0x228` |
| 4 | `0x228` | `0x220` |
| 5 | `0x230` | `0x238` |
| 6 | `0x238` | `0x230` |
| 7 | `0x240` | `0x248` |

That is a separate source inconsistency requiring investigation, not a demonstrated hardware correction.

This was a source review. No files changed, no emulator or gate ran, and the supplied `3/15` measurement was not remeasured.
tokens used
102,220
5 FAULT — reject unsupported widths through the existing MIPS device-failure path.  
CRIME_P1_20260913

This is a model policy choice; the exact hardware response remains unestablished. FAULT reports that the model cannot service the access. Member 3 has a compatibility precedent, but successfully returning zero from a memory/interrupt controller also asserts behavior we have not established.

There is **local evidence omitted from premise 6**: [crimereg.h:39](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/crimereg.h:39) says **“all registers 64-bit access.”** That supports caution about implementing narrower widths, but does not establish the hardware’s rejection behavior. The existing 4-byte compatibility path should remain.

I found no guest driver or PROM instruction sequence in the inspected source establishing byte or halfword CRIME traffic. The relevant evidence was the emulator’s PROM workaround and its comment about NetBSD writing the control register at offset `0x00c`. Absence from this source search does not establish absence from real guests.

There is also a closer structural precedent than the six RE guards: [DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de)](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1182) accepts `{4,8}` and uses a latched decline that explicitly jumps over its switch and drawing operation. That supports skipping operational effects, although it does not settle FAULT versus successful decline.

An eighth combination is **FAULT + LATCHED ANNOUNCE**. Logging and bus disposition are separate choices. I would use member 5’s existing dispatcher diagnostic without introducing another device warning.

For the proposed design, place:

```c
if (len != 4 && len != 8)
	return 0;
```

after the local declarations at line 208, before payload decoding at line 210, and remove the obsolete terminating width arm. Preserve the accepted paths and their common tail.

This placement matters: replacing only the existing `exit(1)` would still execute the preceding REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, and bank-register refreshes.

| Refused operation | Executes inside `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)` |
|---|---|
| 1-byte read | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |
| 2-byte read | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |
| 1-byte write | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |
| 2-byte write | Local initialization, HARDINT snapshot, new guard, `return 0` |

For all four, payload decoding, register refreshes, array access, and the switch are skipped. **None of the common tail executes:** no HARDINT restore, no INTSTAT recomputation, and no `memory_writemax64`. The callback has made no state changes needing cleanup.

The RAM-check statements remain, although unsupported widths now return before reaching them. Those checks remain a separate configuration concern.

For ordinary MIPS data accesses, the dispatcher raises DBE and the load implementation returns before destination-register writeback. However, **“destination untouched” must mean the guest GPR**: this fork [zeroes the transfer buffer before invoking the callback](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485). A sentinel in that buffer cannot distinguish dispositions when testing through `memory_rw`.

The switch effects that member 1 would retain are:

| Exact relative offset | Effect if an unsupported width falls through |
|---|---|
| `0x000`, REV | Redirects the PC in the selected PROM PC window on **reads and writes**. There is no direction check or “second read” counter. |
| `0x008`, CONTROL | Reads do nothing in this arm. Writes inspect raw `idata`, potentially stop CPUs, and may announce unimplemented control bits. |
| `0x010`, INTSTAT | Reads and writes call `crime_interrupt_reassert`. |
| `0x018`, INTMASK | Reads and writes call `crime_interrupt_reassert`. |
| `0x030`, DOG | Breaks without an additional action. |
| `0x038`, TIME | Reads and writes call `crime_update_crime_time`. |
| Other offsets | Execute the default debug output. |

The control-bit arithmetic is explicit: a byte supplies bits `7..0`, maximum `0x00ff`; a halfword supplies bits `15..0`, maximum `0xffff`; HARD_RESET is `0x0800`, bit 11. **A halfword can satisfy that test; a byte cannot.** The switch consumes raw decoded `idata`, without first positioning it into a register byte lane.

These are exact-address cases: `0x004` does not enter REV, and `0x00c` does not enter CONTROL. None of these switch effects belongs on the proposed refused path.

For a detector, I would require the following rows:

- **Controls and survival:** retain `live`, `devsig`, and `devsig hi`; enumerate `lb`, `lbu`, `lh`, `lhu`, `sb`, and `sh` separately. Each needs process survival, absence of the old terminating diagnostic, and a subsequent RAM round trip **after controlled exception recovery**. FAULT intentionally prevents ordinary sequential continuation until the exception is handled.
- **Fault disposition:** check DBE, the faulting EPC under controlled initial exception state, and an unchanged nonzero destination GPR for each of the four loads. Under stepping, check the dispatcher’s failure diagnostic. A surviving process or unchanged GPR alone is insufficient.
- **Accepted accesses:** enumerate 4-byte high-half read, low-half read, high-half write, low-half write, 8-byte read, and 8-byte write. Offset `+0` selects bits `63..32`; `+4` selects bits `31..0`. Use distinct halves, verify preservation of the other half, and check accepted operations both before and after refusals. Include positive checks for HARDINT restoration and INTSTAT recomputation.
- **Refused switch effects:** exercise `{1-read, 2-read, 1-write, 2-write}` at every switch case and a default offset. Observe REV redirection, CPU running flags, interrupt assertion/deassertion, time state, and diagnostics. Prime the interrupt fixture so an erroneous reassert would be observable; control ticks and time so background updates cannot hide or imitate the result.
- **Guard placement:** snapshot CRIME fields directly before and after refusal. Subsequent ordinary CRIME reads themselves refresh several fields and can conceal an incorrectly late guard.
- **Diagnostic policy:** member 5 has no new latch count. If member 3 or the eighth combination is selected, require exactly one announcement per instance across repeated widths and directions, plus an independent announcement from a second instance.

The smallest obvious edit escaping **survival-only** checks is `return 0` → `return 1`. The GPR and DBE rows catch it.

A **one-operator edit that survives all tests restricted to widths `{1,2,4,8}`** is changing the new guard’s first `len != 4` to `len < 4`. Widths 5, 6, and 7 then escape refusal. Thus the callback-level detector also needs unsupported-length cases outside the instruction witness: enumerate 3, 5, 6, 7, and a representative length above 8.

There is another detector limitation worth making explicit: because the dispatcher pre-zeroes read buffers, a successful early `return 1` can look like “the common tail returned zero.” **A guest zero-value row proves successful zero delivery, not execution of the common tail.** Direct callback tests with a seeded buffer distinguish those implementations when tail execution is part of the contract.

The settled-premise audit is:

| Premise | Finding |
|---|---|
| **1** | Confirmed. [fatal](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c:384) prints through `va_debug` and returns. |
| **2** | The normal MIPS exception path is confirmed, with `!no_exceptions` qualification. The “installs ten times” claim is wrong as a runtime statement; see below. |
| **3** | The six RE width declines are present: TLB_A, TLB_B, TLB_C, TEX, LINEAR_A, LINEAR_B. The DE guard is an additional, closer control-flow precedent. |
| **4** | Confirmed: MACEPCI announces and proceeds. This establishes existing behavior, not suitable CRIME semantics. |
| **5** | Confirmed: CRIME has no latch field; the per-instance warning idiom exists. That convention does not itself select a bus disposition. |
| **6** | Incomplete: the bundled header supplies width-related evidence. Exact hardware behavior remains unsettled; PDF absence outside the permitted reading scope was not verified. |
| **7** | Agreed: the RAM failures are outside this width round. RAM size also comes from defaults/configuration, not exclusively `-M`. Setup already rejects values above 1024 MB before registering CRIME. |

Specifically, the ten `unreadable` sites enumerate as:

- IP20: lines **241, 242, 245, 246, 247**, following an unconditional `abort()` at line 220.
- Shared IP22/IP24 branch: lines **345, 346, 349, 350, 351**, following subtype-specific unconditional aborts at lines 268 and 276.
